# LLM inventory from key-papers

Per-paper extraction from `filter/fulltext/*.md`. One row in `filter/llm_models.jsonl` is one experiment (model × method × train data). **OOD is not a clean-generalization certificate** — see below. API and GPT-family rows stay in the jsonl and are omitted from the tables. Numeric OOD gains over the untrained checkpoint and over GRPO: [`llm_gains.md`](llm_gains.md).

## Summary

- Papers read: **247**
- Papers with at least one LLM: **171**
- Papers read but no LLM experiment: **48**
- Distinct model lines: **440**
- Distinct OOD benchmarks: **436**
- Experiment rows with a contamination label: **34**
- Trained rows with no size: **31**
- API / GPT rows omitted from tables: **372**
- Papers with only API / GPT models (hidden): **28**
- Fulltexts excluded (no LLM name, not Post-training/Reasoning): **79**
- Readme entries without fulltext: **55**

## How to read OOD

The OOD column is **not** evidence of clean generalization. It records what a paper evaluated or labeled as out-of-distribution. An alternative explanation for many RLVR math gains — especially on **Qwen2.5-Math-7B** — is that training activates already-learned answers or format, not new reasoning. Spurious reward is a property of the training signal on a checkpoint. Contamination and reward hacking need their own evidence.

Shao et al. ([2506.10947v2](https://arxiv.org/html/2506.10947v2)): on Qwen2.5-Math-7B, MATH-500 rises **+21.4 pp** with a random reward and **+29.1 pp** with a correct reward. The gap between those two gains is not a share of "real reasoning". Comparing only to the untuned checkpoint is not enough to justify a new reward. Yan et al. ([2601.11061v2](https://arxiv.org/html/2601.11061v2)) add mechanistic probes; their **wrong→right** slice is the analysis set, not proof of leakage by itself. Text-recovery and intervention checks are the evidence. Do not copy that conclusion onto every improved item or every Qwen checkpoint.

34 experiment rows below carry a contamination label from those two papers plus a few closely related RLVR setups. An empty Reliability cell means **untested by these sources**, not "clean".

| Checkpoint | Label | What is actually shown |
|---|---|---|
| Qwen2.5-Math-7B | `direct-memorization` | Large gains under random/incorrect reward; mechanistic evidence of retrieving memorized answers on the studied sets. |
| Qwen2.5-Math-1.5B | `spurious-gains` | Spurious-reward gains, reward- and benchmark-dependent. The 7B memorization mechanism is a separate claim. |
| Qwen2.5-1.5B / 7B Base | `sensitivity` | Some uninformative or incorrect signals help; the effect is not the same for every reward. Not a universal contamination finding. |
| Qwen3-8B | `weaker-than-qwen-math` | Yan et al. see similar memorization marks on the studied checkpoint, weaker than Qwen2.5-Math-7B. |
| Qwen2.5-Math-7B-Instruct / Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct | `post-rl-saturated` | Appendix J of Shao et al.: further RLVR, even with a correct reward, adds little. A later post-training regime; a flat curve is not proof the checkpoint is clean. |
| Llama 3.1/3.2 and OLMo 2 in those two papers | `control` | Spurious rewards usually help much less or hurt. Useful controls; not a guarantee of no contamination. |
| Other Qwen3 sizes, OLMo 3, Llama 4, everything else | `untested` | These two papers do not establish a status. |

| Paper | How to read it |
|---|---|
| [One-shot RLVR](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.20571) | High-priority recheck: separate gains from format/entropy controls and from checkpoint picking. The paper already trains Llama, so it is not a Qwen-only result. Default tables use the best average on six benches; Appendix C.1.3 also reports the best score on each benchmark (an optimistic upper bound). |
| [Entropy Minimization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.15134) | Authors already show a much smaller Llama effect and that the result depends on confidence tracking correctness. Keep those on the trained rows; API SciCode names are not the finding. |
| [SKPO](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.08690) | Stronger transfer check than a second public math set: trains Qwen and Llama, evaluates MMLU-Pro and LiveCodeBench. That is not a spurious-reward control. |
| [RLVE](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.07317) | 50 environments unseen in training and 2,500 generated problems are a more contentful transfer control than another public math benchmark. Do not merge those numbers with the DeepMath run. |

Unit of a check is **model × method × train data × eval protocol**, not one OOD bag per paper. Per-paper tables keep one row per experiment. Do not drop Qwen papers; do not treat their OOD lists as clean-generalization certificates.

## Per-paper

### Reinforcement learning

| Paper | Model | Method | Train | ID | OOD | Reliability | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| [CDE: Curiosity-Driven Exploration for RL in LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.09675) | Qwen3-4B-Base (base, trained) | CDE | DAPO-17K | MATH, AMC 2023, AIME 2024, AIME 2025 | GPQA | ood: paper | CDE on GRPO (actor PPL bonus) and PPO (multi-head critic bonus) vs vanilla GRPO/PPO. Paper labels AMC23 in-domain and GPQA OOD; MATH/AIME24/AIME25 are the other main math evals. |
| [1000 Layer Networks for Self-Supervised RL (NeurIPS 2025 Best Paper)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.14858) | — | — | — | — | — | — | Classic self-supervised contrastive RL on JaxGCRL locomotion/manipulation; Llama 3 cited only as a depth analogy. No LLM experiment. |
| [Meta-Reinforcement Learning with Zero-Shot RL](https://openreview.net/forum?id=XyGJJ4FPoX) | — | — | — | — | — | — | Meta-RL / BFM / ZSRL on toy domains, MetaWorld, and humanoid; LLM-assisted coding is a related-work citation only. No LLM experiment. |
| [Sample-Efficient RL by Breaking the Replay Ratio Barrier (ICLR 2023, precursor of BBF)](https://openreview.net/forum?id=OpC-9aBBVJe) | — | — | — | — | — | — | Classic deep RL (replay-ratio / reset) on Atari 100k and DeepMind Control Suite. No LLM experiment. |
| [A Minimalist Approach to Offline RL (TD3+BC)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2106.06860) | — | — | — | — | — | — | Offline RL (TD3+BC) on D4RL; Decision Transformer is an offline-RL baseline, not an LLM. No LLM experiment. |
| [Offline RL: Tutorial, Review, and Perspectives](https://arxiv.org/abs/2005.01643) | — | — | — | — | — | — | Tutorial/review of offline RL (2020); no LLM experiments. |
| [A Distributional Perspective on RL (C51)](https://arxiv.org/abs/1707.06887) | — | — | — | — | — | — | Classic distributional RL (C51) on Atari/ALE; no LLM experiments. |
| [Prioritized Experience Replay](https://arxiv.org/abs/1511.05952) | — | — | — | — | — | — | DQN / Double DQN with prioritized replay on Atari 2600; no LLM experiments. |

| [The Llama 3 Herd of Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.21783) | Llama 3 (8B,70B,405B, base, introduced) | pretrain + long-context pretrain + annealing (Polyak checkpoint average) | 15.6T multilingual tokens to end of 2023 (mix ~50% general knowledge, 25% math/reasoning, 17% code, 8% multilingual; web + domain code/math pipelines), long-context continued pretrain ~800B tokens, 8K→128K in six stages, annealing last 40M tokens: upsample high-quality code and mathematical data (no common benchmark train sets) |  | MMLU, MMLU-Pro, ARC Challenge, HellaSwag, PiQA, Natural Questions, SQuAD, QQP +8 | ood: paper | Paper: all reported numbers are Llama 3.1, called Llama 3 for brevity. Dense Transformer + GQA (8 KV heads), 128K tiktoken-based vocab, RoPE base 500k, document-masked attention. 405B: 126 layers, dim 16384, 128 heads; 3.8e25 FLOPs. Smaller models trained far past compute-optimal. Annealing text: assess few-shot and out-of-domain generalization. Tables 8/10–15; robustness on MMLU. |

Also used iteratively as synthetic-data teacher and Llama-based quality/difficulty/code judge. |

Validation perplexity vs AdamW: 4.18 vs 4.25 (small), 3.42 vs 3.54 (medium), 3.18 vs 3.25 (large). |

Paper writes three models ranging from 1.1B to 7.5B and names 1.1B, 2.1B, 7.5B. Adafactor is the large-scale baseline (no perplexity gap; Lion better on average ICL, Table 5). Appendix C lists 3 NLG + the NLU tasks above (paper says 21 NLU). |

| [Emu: Enhancing Image Generation with Photogenic Needles](https://arxiv.org/abs/2309.15807) | — | — | — | — | — | — | Text-to-image quality-tuning of a 2.8B U-Net LDM (plus pixel-diffusion / Muse-like ablations). CLIP ViT-L and T5-XXL used only as frozen text encoders; Llama2/ChatGPT cited as analogy. No LLM training or language-model eval. |
| [To Compress or Not to Compress: SSL and Information Theory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2304.09355) | — | — | — | — | — | — | Survey/review of SSL through information theory (InfoMax, IB, MINE). Cites BERT as a denoising-autoencoder example. No original LLM training or evaluation runs. |
| [DINOv2](https://arxiv.org/abs/2304.07193) | — | — | — | — | — | — | Vision SSL: trains DINOv2 ViT backbones (up to ViT-g, 1B) on LVD-142M. CLIP/OpenCLIP appear only as frozen vision-feature baselines. NLP LMs (GPT-3, PaLM, Llama) cited as motivation; no language-model experiment. |
| [ImageReward](https://arxiv.org/abs/2304.05977) | — | — | — | — | — | — | Text-to-image reward model (BLIP ViT-L backbone) and ReFL fine-tuning of Stable Diffusion v1.4. Sentence-BERT used only to cluster DiffusionDB prompts; a BERT token-classifier is a prompt-analysis tool. InstructGPT/GPT-3 appear only in related work. No LLM experiment. |
| [BEiT-3: Image as a Foreign Language](https://arxiv.org/abs/2208.10442) | — | — | — | — | — | — | Introduces BEiT-3, a 1.9B Multiway Transformer VLM pretrained with masked data modeling on images, texts, and image-text pairs. Eval is vision/VL only (COCO, ADE20K, ImageNet, VQAv2, NLVR2, Flickr30K). No language-only LLM experiment. |
| [A Path Towards Autonomous Machine Intelligence (LeCun, 2022)](https://openreview.net/forum?id=BZ5a1r-kVsf) | — | — | — | — | — | — | Architecture proposal (JEPA / H-JEPA, Mode-1/Mode-2 actor). Discusses LLMs conceptually as scaling-is-not-enough; no training or evaluation runs. |

Frozen LMs; sparse autoencoders trained on cached residual-stream (and MLP) activations from the Pile (5–50M vectors, 1–3 epochs, Adam 1e-3). IOI feature patching vs PCA; Appendix F is Pythia-410M. Layer-2 Pythia-70M reconstruction raises Pile perplexity 25→40. No LM weight training. |

No LLM training or benchmark runs. |

## Excluded fulltexts (no LLM name)
